<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, March 8, 2016 
 
PRESENT:  Jason Whiting, Chairman; Jesse Thompson, Vice Chairman; Steve Williams, Supervisor; Robert 
Black, Jr., Supervisor; and Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor. 
9:02 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation was led by Supervisor Thompson.  
CALL TO THE PUBLIC:  Terry Hill, 2677 Arizona Highway 77 in Shumway , Commander of Veterans of 
Foreign Wars Post 907 in Show Low, gave an update on the Honor Flight for Major Tina Welsh and stated that 
over $2,000.00 was raised for her flight and an escort, for a wheelchair when she needs it, and motel room. He 
thanked members of the Board and County who donated, especially Supervisor Williams for his generous 
donation of $200.00 out of his fund . He further commented on the veterans in our area.  He spoke of a young 
combat medic who suffered severely from PTSD who recently lost her life and asked that we all remember those 
veterans.  He spoke about the need for mental health care for the young veterans who are coming home.  Dr. 
Richard Petke, a naturopathic physician from Flagstaff, asked about the current open position of Health Director. 
He spoke of his qualifications and stated that he applied for the position on October 23 rd and has not heard 
anything on it . Chairman Whiting asked Mr. Bryan L ayton to speak with Dr. Rich ard Petke. Bryan Layton 
introduced Adam Wolfe , the new Communications Manager, and spoke of his qualifications. The Board 
welcomed Adam to the Navajo County family.  
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes: February 23, 2016 Regular Meeting  b) Warrant & Voucher Report over 
$1000 February 2016  c) Constable Reports: Winslow Precinct #2, February 2016; Snowflake Precinct #3, 
November 2015; and Show Low Precinct #5, February 2016  d) Approval of 4 Back Tax Land Parcels sold over 
the counter in the amount of $6,152.12 e) Grant Award Contract for Grant No. CNA16-302 for one constable to 
attend outside training  f) Memorandum of Understanding with the City of Show Low to allow Emergency 
Preparedness to deliver medical countermeasures  g) Memorandum of Understanding with Summit Healthcare 
Regional Medical Center for a closed Point of Destination h) Cooperative Law Enforcement Agreement with 
USDA Forest Service contract #12 -LE-11030121-002 Operating and Financial Plan for $11,205.16 for law 
enforcement cooperative efforts for FY16  i) Amendment 4 to IGA #120067DC with the Department of 
Corrections for correctional officer certification training  j) Name an unnamed existing road in the 
“SNOWFLAKE” area to “DESERT STAR ROAD”;  in Section 23 ; Township 14 North ; Range 22 East  k) 
Attachment E to the Master Intergovernmental Agreement CO10070 with the Navajo Nation and Navajo County 
for Road Projects l) Reappointment of Glen Johnson to the Board of Adjustment for an additional four-year term 
to expire in 2020 m) Sympathy Letter for Bob Damon, former Board of Supervisor , Santa Cruz County; and 
Kevin Brackney, former Superintendent of Schools, Show Low  n) Letter of Support for the TechHire Grant 
Opportunity: Supervisor Williams made a motion to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the consent 
agenda; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:   
a. Presentation to Jackson Allen for winning the Division III State Wrestling Championship in his weight 
class and Brock Slade and Baylend Stephens for winning the Division IV State Wrestling Championship 
in their respective weight classes : Supervisor William s recognized Jackson Allen , Show Low High 
School, for winning the Division III State Wrestling Championship in his weight class. Jackson indicated 
that his favorite take down is the double leg and that participating in wrestling has taught him leadership 
skills in and out of wrestling and setting an example to younger kids.  Supervisor Williams acknowledged

<!-- page 2 -->
Brock Slade and Baylend Stephens, Mogollon High School, for winning the Division IV State Wrestling 
Championship in their respective weight classes. 
b. Presentation to Adam Henderson for winning the Division III State Wrestling Championship in his weight 
class: Supervisor Whitesinger recognized Adam Henderson for winning the Division III State Wrestling 
Championship in his weight class  and acknowledged the d edication and commitment that winning a 
championship requires. Adam Henderson indicated that he likes whatever takedown is available, but his 
favorite is the double leg.  Mr. Henderson stated that he is looking at a career in engineering or the medical 
field. He stated that through participating in wrestling, he has learned  discipline and learning to control 
himself and that there are no easy days.  
c. Presentation to Cole Mortensen for winning the Division IV State Wrestling Championship in his weight 
class: Supervisor Whiting  recognized Cole Mortensen for winning the Division IV State Wrestling 
Championship in his weight class.  Supervisor Whiting also commended the youth in our area for their 
involvement in their communities.  Cole stated that his favorite takedown is the boot scoot.  He stated that 
wrestling has taught him personal responsibility.  
Supervisor Thompson commended the wrestlers for their dedication and good example.  
 
PUBLIC WORKS: NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT:  Board of Directors Session:   
a. Consideration and possible approval of $5,000.00 to Little Colorado River Plateau RC&D to support weed 
control/management in Rainbow Lake for tourism, recreation, fishing, s wimming and boating : 
Supervisor Williams made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Flood Control District Board of 
Directors session; motion seconded by Supervisor Black . Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
Supervisor Whitesinger introduced the Little Colorado River Plateau RC&D and Rainbow Lake and the 
continued Board support for removal of invasive weeds. She spoke about releasing carp into the lake as a 
method of removing the weeds. Bill Bess requested the approval of this item. Chairman Whiting thanked 
Supervisor Whitesinger for her leadership in her community. Supervisor Williams made a motion to 
approve $5,000.00 to Little Colorado River Plateau RC&D to support weed control/management in 
Rainbow Lake for tourism, recreation, fishing, swimming a nd boating; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Supervisor Williams made a motion to return to regular 
session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
2) PUSH PROGRAM: Presentation and updates from the Navajo County PUSH (People United Supporting 
Health) Program (Wellness Committee) item pulled 
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions: Laura Carrell reviewed the 16-item Personnel 
Action Report . Supervisor Thompson  made a motion  to approve the Personnel Action Report as 
presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger.   Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of Northern Arizona Council of Governments Techni cal Assistance 
Contract to provide technical assistance in administering the Community Development Block Grant 
recently awarded to Navajo County for th e 911 Equipment Upgrade project:  Olivia Todd discussed the 
item.  Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve the Northern Arizona Council of Governments 
Technical Assistance Contract to provide technical assistance in administering the Community 
Development Block Grant recently awarded to Navajo County for the 911 Equipment Upgrade project ; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 3 -->
COUNTY ATTORNEY/SHERIFF: Presentation of the Jail-Based Treatment Program , its successes and the 
value it has on the communities within Navajo County : Sheriff Clark presented on the Jail Based Treatment 
program which is partnered with CBI, Community Bridges, Inc., which includes in-house treatment followed by 
intensive out -patient treatment. Sheriff Clark stated that the inmates involved in the program have taken 
ownership of the program.  Karie Peel, from CBI , spoke about the Jail Based Treatment Program  and the 
background of the referral process. She spoke about how the participants are assessed on six dimensions, based 
on the American Society of Addiction Medicine.  She spoke about how the inmates have taken ownership of the 
program and the room itself as far as cleaning and how they have requested homework.  She spoke about some 
national statistics for relapse rates for people with just a drug addiction problem and people who have a drug  
addiction problem along with other chronic illnesses. She indicated that CBI has served 29 inmates and only two 
have relapsed and out of those two who relapsed, one returned to the jail. She spoke about the Happiness Scale, 
Dialectical Behavior Therapy, and the Awareness Scale.  An inmate currently participating in the Program spoke 
about what he is learning from this program and that the program has given him hope, and he is very grateful for 
this program. Mr. Carlyon asked some questions of the inmate.  Patrick Sample stated that CBI is one of the best 
things that has happened in his life.  He spoke about the wise mind, which is the reasonable and the emotional 
mind. He spoke about the skills that he has learned and hopes that one day he can work with CBI and help others.  
Mr. Carlyon asked some questions of Patrick Sample.  Adrian Milligan spoke about the program and the life skills 
that he has learned from CBI. Mr. Carlyon asked some questions of Adrian  Milligan. Sheriff Clark spoke about 
Navajo County statistics and how they measure up to other counties. Heather Rogers, counselor, spoke about the 
start of the program  and how it has progressed through participant feedback . Jason Moore, Deputy County 
Attorney, spoke about the South County Drug Court Program  and the past struggle in finding suitable inpatient 
treatment for some participants and how CBI has provided an option for the harder cases . Karie Peel stated that 
the inpatient program is a minimum of 90 da ys, and once they graduate from that program, they go to a more 
intensive outpatient program until they meet all criteria at which point they slowly step down .  Brandt Clark, 
Deputy County Attorney, spoke about the North County Drug Court Program  and the benefit of having services 
available locally. Shanda Breed, Probation Director, spoke about our children and drug and alcohol addiction and 
the challenge of people who finish treatment and return to their communities and how the program is incorporating 
a plan for the participants who are on probation to prepare to leave the program . She spoke about the success 
stories from this program. Brad Carlyon spoke about the Drug Court Program and the CBI Jail Based Treatment 
Program and the services that both progra ms provide and the effect substance abuse has on families and the 
communities. Supervisor Thompson  commended Brad Carlyon and the others who have worked with this 
program. Brad Carlyon answered questions of the Board stating that there are several partners that work with CBI 
and Drug Court for GED’s, jobs, medical, etc. Brad Carlyon stated that substance abuse is a public health issue.  
Supervisor Thompson stated that he is very supportive of this program. Supervisor Williams gave a quote 
regarding how people can change and commended the participants of the program who came forward and wished 
them luck as they continue on. Supervisor Whitesinger thanked Mr. Carlyon and Sheriff Clark for their efforts 
in championing such a great cause. She stated her support of the program. Supervisor Black commended those 
who have been involved in these programs. Chairman Whiting thanked all those involved in trying to find better 
ways to serve our citizens. 
EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT: Consideration and possible approval of the Hazardous Materials Emergency 
Planning Grant Award: Catrina Jenkins discussed the item.  Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve 
the Hazardous Materials Emergency Planning Grant Award ; motion seconded by Supervisor Black . Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  Mary Springer gave an up date on the Phoenix Fire near A ripine. She further 
spoke about the dangers of fire season and the need to raise people’s awareness level.

<!-- page 4 -->
WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES COUNTY RECREATION IMPROVEMENT DIS TRICT: Board of 
Directors Session:  Supervisor Williams  made a motion to enter into the White Mountain Lakes County 
Recreation Improvement District Board of Directors Session ; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Consideration and possible approval to amend the By-Laws (Resolution 
Number 36-15) to remove the word (Resident) and the definition of, from the By -Laws and replace it with (Tax 
Payer) and to include the definition of “Tax Payer”, as one who pays taxes to the White Mountain Lake County 
Recreation Improvement District: Jerry McGraw discussed the item  and stated that it has been reviewed by the 
attorney. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve to amend the By-Laws (Resolution Number 36-15) 
to remove the word (Resident) and the definition of, from the By -Laws, and replace it with (Tax Payer) and to 
include the definition of “Tax Payer”, as one who pays taxes to the White Mountain Lake County Recreation 
Improvement District; motion seconded by Supervisor Wh itesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
Chairman Whiting  thanked Mr. McGraw for his hard work and dedication to White Mountain Lakes . 
Supervisor Williams made a motion to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE:  Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Thompson gave an update on his trip to Washington DC for the NACo meetings regarding improving 
School Bus Routes on the res ervations. Supervisor Thompson  left at 10:44 a.m. Supervisor Williams said 
Happy Birthday to Barbara Bruce , whose birthday was yesterday. Supervisor Black gave an update on his trip 
to Washington DC for the NACo meetings and that he is a member of the Environment, Energy and Land Use  
Committee and the issues with which that committee is dealing. He reported on his meeting with last year’s NACo 
president.  He spoke about the initiation of the next phase of the Red Dog Project.  Supervisor Black thanked 
Eric Oscarson for all that he has done. He spoke about the Red Dog Project and Navajo County hosting the 
Western Agency Council meeting in June. Supervisor Whitesinger spoke about the White Mountain Regional 
Transportation Committee meeting and the several townships who attended to talk about regional transportation 
needs, the needs for dollars, and how to advocate for those dollars and ensure that the Mountain is part of the 
conversation.  She thanked the staff that support the White Mountain Regional Transportation Committee.  She 
stated that one of the goals for the upcoming meeting is for every township and every committee to bring a project 
that is being put on the side that they want to see prioritized.  She spoke of continued conversation regar ding 
Route 12 out to Cibecue.  She spoke of reading for Read Across America at Cradleboard Elementary School. She 
reported on a sign-up event for Ready Navajo County that occurred in Cibecue over the past weekend. She stated 
that she had a site visit at Sunrise and that they have 12 platforms almost complete for the zip line.  She spoke of 
her attendance at the Substance Abuse Prevention, New Paradigm and New Challenges and encouraged everyone 
to do their own research and investigation before making their d ecision regarding the legalization of marijuana. 
Chairman Whiting  thanked Barbara Bruce for all her work on the different committees with which she is 
involved and wished her a very Happy Birthday. He thanked the fellow Board members for their support on the 
issues going on in the forest. He spoke about the challenge of the existing timber industry surviving before 4FRI 
comes.  He spoke about his meetings with the regional forester and looking for ways to get wood to the industry 
now.  He spoke about his meetings with Chief Tidwell in Washington. He further thanked Mr. Jayne and the team 
support on some of the cost shifts that have happened. He spoke about the cost shifts and how the State needs to 
begin to make the County whole. He spoke of the conversation s with legislators regarding these cost shifts  and 
the State taking back those areas that the State structurally shifted the cost to us that are not our responsibility. He 
thanked the representatives that heard from us regarding these cost shifts and spoke  of a letter of support from 
Senator Allen which she will be taking to leadership. He stated that the wolves will be released in Navajo County

<!-- page 5 -->
and the negative impact that this release will cause. He spoke about the good youth in our area and shared the 
example of a local high school basketball team who took widows out to dinner for Valentine’s Day. He spoke 
about our children leaving our community for opportunities and what we can do keep them here.  
ADJOURN at 11:10 a.m.  
APPROVED:    
 
 
 
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
              DATED: March 8, 2016
